# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in <Operations> with a specfication.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title employee Model

class Company {
    name : String
    departments : List<Department>
    employees : List<Employee>
    calculateTotalEmployeeSalary() : double
    calculateTotalSalesPeopleCommission() : double
    calculateTotalShiftWorkerHolidayPremiums() : double
}
class Department {
    type : DepartmentType
    company : Company
    employees : List<Employee>
    manager : Manager
    calculateAverageWorkerWorkingHours() : double
}
abstract class Employee {
    birthDate : EDate
    department : String
    name : String
    socialInsuranceNumber : String
    company : Company
}
class Manager {
    position : String
    salary : double
    subordinates : List<Employee>
    getDirectSubordinateEmployeesCount() : int
}
class OffShiftWorker {
    officialHolidayPermit : boolean
    weekendPermit : boolean
}
class SalesPeople {
    amountOfSales : double
    commissionPercentage : double
    salary : double
    getTotalCommission() : double
}
class ShiftWorker {
    holidayPremium : double
    calculateHolidayPremium() : double
    setdepartment(department : String) : void
}
abstract class Worker {
    hourlyRates : double
    weeklyWorkingHour : int
}
enum DepartmentType {
    PRODUCTION
    CONTROL
    DELIVERY
}

Company *-- "*" Department : departments  'opposite: company
Company *-- "*" Employee : employees  'opposite: company
Department --> Company : company  'opposite: departments
Department --> "*" Employee : employees
Department --> Manager : manager
Employee --> Company : company  'opposite: employees
Manager --|> Employee
Manager --> "*" Employee : subordinates
OffShiftWorker --|> Worker
SalesPeople --|> Employee
ShiftWorker --|> Worker
Worker --|> Employee
@enduml
```

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title employee Model

class Company {
    name : String
    departments : List<Department>
    employees : List<Employee>
    calculateTotalEmployeeSalary() : double
    calculateTotalSalesPeopleCommission() : double
    calculateTotalShiftWorkerHolidayPremiums() : double
}
class Department {
    type : DepartmentType
    company : Company
    employees : List<Employee>
    manager : Manager
    calculateAverageWorkerWorkingHours() : double
}
abstract class Employee {
    birthDate : EDate
    department : String
    name : String
    socialInsuranceNumber : String
    company : Company
}
class Manager {
    position : String
    salary : double
    subordinates : List<Employee>
    getDirectSubordinateEmployeesCount() : int
}
class OffShiftWorker {
    officialHolidayPermit : boolean
    weekendPermit : boolean
}
class SalesPeople {
    amountOfSales : double
    commissionPercentage : double
    salary : double
    getTotalCommission() : double
}
class ShiftWorker {
    holidayPremium : double
    calculateHolidayPremium() : double
    setdepartment(department : String) : void
}
abstract class Worker {
    hourlyRates : double
    weeklyWorkingHour : int
}
enum DepartmentType {
    PRODUCTION
    CONTROL
    DELIVERY
}

Company *-- "*" Department : departments  'opposite: company
Company *-- "*" Employee : employees  'opposite: company
Department --> Company : company  'opposite: departments
Department --> "*" Employee : employees
Department --> Manager : manager
Employee --> Company : company  'opposite: employees
Manager --|> Employee
Manager --> "*" Employee : subordinates
OffShiftWorker --|> Worker
SalesPeople --|> Employee
ShiftWorker --|> Worker
Worker --|> Employee
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Company::calculateTotalEmployeeSalary
Summary: Calculates the total salary of all employees in the company.
Algorithm: 
  1. Initialize totalSalary = 0.0
  2. For each employee in this.employees:
     - If employee is Worker:
         salary = weeklyWorkingHour * hourlyRates
         If employee is ShiftWorker: salary += holidayPremium
     - Else if employee is SalesPeople:
         salary = salary + (amountOfSales * commissionPercentage)
     - Else if employee is Manager:
         salary = salary (fixed salary)
     - totalSalary += salary
  3. Return round(totalSalary, 2)
Related Features: this.employees
Related Classes: Employee, Worker, ShiftWorker, SalesPeople, Manager
Input: None
Output: The total salary of all employees, rounded to two decimal places, as a double.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: The total salary is computed and returned rounded to two decimal places.

Company::calculateTotalSalesPeopleCommission
Summary: Calculates the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize totalCommission = 0.0
  2. For each employee in this.employees:
     - If employee is SalesPeople:
         totalCommission += (amountOfSales * commissionPercentage)
  3. Return round(totalCommission, 2)
Related Features: this.employees
Related Classes: Employee, SalesPeople
Input: None
Output: Total commission amount for all salespeople, rounded to two decimal places, as a double.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: The total commission is computed and returned rounded to two decimal places.

Company::calculateTotalShiftWorkerHolidayPremiums
Summary: Calculates the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize totalPremium = 0.0
  2. For each employee in this.employees:
     - If employee is ShiftWorker:
         totalPremium += holidayPremium
  3. If totalPremium == 0.0, return 0.0
  4. Return round(totalPremium, 2)
Related Features: this.employees
Related Classes: Employee, ShiftWorker
Input: None
Output: Total holiday premiums for all shift workers, rounded to two decimal places, as a double. Returns 0.0 if no shift workers exist.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: The total holiday premium is computed and returned rounded to two decimal places. Returns 0.0 if there are no shift workers.

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

# Operation Specifications
Department::calculateAverageWorkerWorkingHours
Summary: Calculates the average weekly working hours for all workers in this department.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize totalHours = 0, workerCount = 0
  2. For each employee in this.employees:
     - If employee is Worker:
         totalHours += weeklyWorkingHour
         workerCount++
  3. If workerCount == 0, return 0.0
  4. average = totalHours / workerCount
  5. Return round(average, 2)
Related Features: this.employees
Related Classes: Employee, Worker
Input: None
Output: Average weekly working hours of workers in the department, rounded to two decimal places, as a double. Returns 0.0 if no workers exist.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: The average is computed and returned rounded to two decimal places. Returns 0.0 if no workers in department.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

   * Summary: Calculates the average weekly working hours for all workers in this department.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Initialize totalHours = 0, workerCount = 0
   *   2. For each employee in this.employees:
   *      - If employee is Worker:
   *          totalHours += employee.weeklyWorkingHour
   *          workerCount++
   *   3. If workerCount == 0, return 0.0
   *   4. average = totalHours / workerCount
   *   5. Return round(average, 2)
   * Related Features: this.employees
   * Related Classes: Employee, Worker
   * Input: None
   * Output: Average weekly working hours of workers in the department, rounded to two decimal places, as a double. Returns 0.0 if no workers exist.

   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public double calculateAverageWorkerWorkingHours()
  {
    int totalHours = 0;
    int workerCount = 0;
    for (Employee employee : getEmployees())
    {
      if (employee instanceof Worker)
      {
        totalHours += ((Worker) employee).getWeeklyWorkingHour();
        workerCount++;
      }
    }
    if (workerCount == 0)
    {
      return 0.0;
    }
    double average = (double) totalHours / workerCount;
    return Math.round(average * 100.0) / 100.0;
  }

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Navigate the Ecore model to find related classes
  1.2 Also consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.3 Locate these files in your project.
  1.4 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the **Context**.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

```java
public double calculateTotalEmployeeSalary()
{
    double totalSalary = 0.0;
    for (Employee employee : this.employees) {
        double salary = 0.0;
        if (employee instanceof Worker) {
            Worker worker = (Worker) employee;
            salary = worker.getWeeklyWorkingHour() * worker.getHourlyRates();
            if (employee instanceof ShiftWorker) {
                ShiftWorker shiftWorker = (ShiftWorker) employee;
                salary += shiftWorker.getHolidayPremium();
            }
        } else if (employee instanceof SalesPeople) {
            SalesPeople salesPeople = (SalesPeople) employee;
            salary = salesPeople.getSalary() + (salesPeople.getAmountOfSales() * salesPeople.getCommissionPercentage());
        } else if (employee instanceof Manager) {
            Manager manager = (Manager) employee;
            salary = manager.getSalary();
        }
        totalSalary += salary;
    }
    return Math.round(totalSalary * 100.0) / 100.0;
}
```

Wait, I need to be careful. The `instanceof` check for ShiftWorker should be inside the Worker check since ShiftWorker extends Worker. Let me re-read the algorithm:

```
- If employee is Worker:
    salary = weeklyWorkingHour * hourlyRates
    If employee is ShiftWorker: salary += holidayPremium
```

So the ShiftWorker check is nested inside the Worker check. That makes sense since ShiftWorker extends Worker. But actually, in Java, `instanceof Worker` will also match ShiftWorker. So the logic is correct.

But wait - what about OffShiftWorker? It also extends Worker. The algorithm only says "If employee is Worker" and then "If employee is ShiftWorker: salary += holidayPremium". OffShiftWorker doesn't have holidayPremium, so it just gets the base Worker salary.

Now let me also check if there's a `SalesPeople.getTotalCommission()` method. Looking at the summary: `public double getTotalCommission()`. public double calculateTotalEmployeeSalary()
{
    double totalSalary = 0.0;
    for (Employee employee : this.employees) {
        double salary = 0.0;
        if (employee instanceof Worker) {
            Worker worker = (Worker) employee;
            salary = worker.getWeeklyWorkingHour() * worker.getHourlyRates();
            if (employee instanceof ShiftWorker) {
                ShiftWorker shiftWorker = (ShiftWorker) employee;
                salary += shiftWorker.getHolidayPremium();
            }
        } else if (employee instanceof SalesPeople) {
            SalesPeople salesPeople = (SalesPeople) employee;
            salary = salesPeople.getSalary() + (salesPeople.getAmountOfSales() * salesPeople.getCommissionPercentage());
        } else if (employee instanceof Manager) {
            Manager manager = (Manager) employee;
            salary = manager.getSalary();
        }
        totalSalary += salary;
    }
    return Math.round(totalSalary * 100.0) / 100.0;
}

   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->

   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Calculates the total salary of all employees in the company.
   * Algorithm: 
   *   1. Initialize totalSalary = 0.0
   *   2. For each employee in this.employees:
   *      - If employee is Worker:
   *          salary = weeklyWorkingHour * hourlyRates
   *          If employee is ShiftWorker: salary += holidayPremium
   *      - Else if employee is SalesPeople:
   *          salary = salary + (amountOfSales * commissionPercentage)
   *      - Else if employee is Manager:
   *          salary = salary (fixed salary)
   *      - totalSalary += salary
   *   3. Return round(totalSalary, 2)
   * Related Features: this.employees
   * Related Classes: Employee, Worker, ShiftWorker, SalesPeople, Manager
   * Input: None
   * Output: The total salary of all employees, rounded to two decimal places, as a double.
   * Pre-condition: None
   * Post-condition: The total salary is computed and returned rounded to two decimal places.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public double calculateTotalEmployeeSalary()
  {
    double totalSalary = 0.0;
    for (Employee employee : getEmployees()) {
      double salary = 0.0;
      if (employee instanceof Worker) {
        Worker worker = (Worker) employee;
        salary = worker.getWeeklyWorkingHour() * worker.getHourlyRates();
        if (employee instanceof ShiftWorker) {
          salary += ((ShiftWorker) employee).getHolidayPremium();
        }
      } else if (employee instanceof SalesPeople) {
        SalesPeople salesPeople = (SalesPeople) employee;
        salary = salesPeople.getSalary() + (salesPeople.getAmountOfSales() * salesPeople.getCommissionPercentage());
      } else if (employee instanceof Manager) {
        Manager manager = (Manager) employee;
        salary = manager.getSalary();
      }
      totalSalary += salary;
    }
    return Math.round(totalSalary * 100.0) / 100.0;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Calculates the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Initialize totalCommission = 0.0
   *   2. For each employee in this.employees:
   *      - If employee is SalesPeople:
   *          totalCommission += (amountOfSales * commissionPercentage)
   *   3. Return round(totalCommission, 2)
   * Related Features: this.employees
   * Related Classes: Employee, SalesPeople
   * Input: None
   * Output: Total commission amount for all salespeople, rounded to two decimal places, as a double.
   * Pre-condition: None
   * Post-condition: The total commission is computed and returned rounded to two decimal places.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public double calculateTotalSalesPeopleCommission()
  {
    double totalCommission = 0.0;
    for (Employee employee : getEmployees()) {
      if (employee instanceof SalesPeople) {
        SalesPeople salesPeople = (SalesPeople) employee;
        totalCommission += salesPeople.getAmountOfSales() * salesPeople.getCommissionPercentage();
      }
    }
    return Math.round(totalCommission * 100.0) / 100.0;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Calculates the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Initialize totalPremium = 0.0
   *   2. For each employee in this.employees:
   *      - If employee is ShiftWorker:
   *          totalPremium += holidayPremium
   *   3. Return round(totalPremium, 2)
   * Related Features: this.employees
   * Related Classes: Employee, ShiftWorker
   * Input: None
   * Output: Total holiday premiums for all shift workers, rounded to two decimal places, as a double.
   * Pre-condition: None
   * Post-condition: The total holiday premium is computed and returned rounded to two decimal places.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public double calculateTotalShiftWorkerHolidayPremiums()
  {
    double totalPremium = 0.0;
    for (Employee employee : getEmployees()) {
      if (employee instanceof ShiftWorker) {
        ShiftWorker shiftWorker = (ShiftWorker) employee;
        totalPremium += shiftWorker.getHolidayPremium();
      }
    }
    return Math.round(totalPremium * 100.0) / 100.0;
  }

```java
public double calculateTotalEmployeeSalary()
{
    double totalSalary = 0.0;
    for (Employee employee : this.employees) {
        double salary = 0.0;
        if (employee instanceof Worker) {
            Worker worker = (Worker) employee;
            salary = worker.getWeeklyWorkingHour() * worker.getHourlyRates();
            if (employee instanceof ShiftWorker) {
                ShiftWorker shiftWorker = (ShiftWorker) employee;
                salary += shiftWorker.getHolidayPremium();
            }
        } else if (employee instanceof SalesPeople) {
            SalesPeople salesPeople = (SalesPeople) employee;
            salary = salesPeople.getSalary() + (salesPeople.getAmountOfSales() * salesPeople.getCommissionPercentage());
        } else if (employee instanceof Manager) {
            Manager manager = (Manager) employee;
            salary = manager.getSalary();
        }
        totalSalary += salary;
    }
    return Math.round(totalSalary * 100.0) / 100.0;
}
```

```
- If employee is Worker:
    salary = weeklyWorkingHour * hourlyRates
    If employee is ShiftWorker: salary += holidayPremium
```

So the ShiftWorker check is nested inside the Worker check. That makes sense since ShiftWorker extends Worker. But actually, in Java, `instanceof Worker` will also match ShiftWorker. So the logic is correct.

          salary += ((ShiftWorker) employee).getHolidayPremium();
        }
      } else if (employee instanceof SalesPeople) {
        SalesPeople salesPeople = (SalesPeople) employee;
        salary = salesPeople.getSalary() + (salesPeople.getAmountOfSales() * salesPeople.getCommissionPercentage());
      } else if (employee instanceof Manager) {
        Manager manager = (Manager) employee;
        salary = manager.getSalary();
      }
      totalSalary += salary;
    }
    return Math.round(totalSalary * 100.0) / 100.0;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Calculates the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Initialize totalCommission = 0.0
   *   2. For each employee in this.employees:
   *      - If employee is SalesPeople:
   *          totalCommission += (amountOfSales * commissionPercentage)
   *   3. Return round(totalCommission, 2)
   * Related Features: this.employees
   * Related Classes: Employee, SalesPeople
   * Input: None
   * Output: Total commission amount for all salespeople, rounded to two decimal places, as a double.
   * Pre-condition: None
   * Post-condition: The total commission is computed and returned rounded to two decimal places.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public double calculateTotalSalesPeopleCommission()
  {
    double totalCommission = 0.0;
    for (Employee employee : getEmployees()) {
      if (employee instanceof SalesPeople) {
        SalesPeople salesPeople = (SalesPeople) employee;
        totalCommission += salesPeople.getAmountOfSales() * salesPeople.getCommissionPercentage();
      }
    }
    return Math.round(totalCommission * 100.0) / 100.0;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Calculates the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Initialize totalPremium = 0.0
   *   2. For each employee in this.employees:
   *      - If employee is ShiftWorker:
   *          totalPremium += holidayPremium
   *   3. Return round(totalPremium, 2)
   * Related Features: this.employees
   * Related Classes: Employee, ShiftWorker
   * Input: None
   * Output: Total holiday premiums for all shift workers, rounded to two decimal places, as a double.
   * Pre-condition: None
   * Post-condition: The total holiday premium is computed and returned rounded to two decimal places.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public double calculateTotalShiftWorkerHolidayPremiums()
  {
    double totalPremium = 0.0;
    for (Employee employee : getEmployees()) {
      if (employee instanceof ShiftWorker) {
        ShiftWorker shiftWorker = (ShiftWorker) employee;
        totalPremium += shiftWorker.getHolidayPremium();
      }
    }
    return Math.round(totalPremium * 100.0) / 100.0;
  }

      case EmployeePackage.COMPANY__NAME:

  }

  @SuppressWarnings("unchecked")
  @Override
  public void eSet(int featureID, Object newValue)
  {
    switch (featureID)
    {
      case EmployeePackage.COMPANY__DEPARTMENTS:
        getDepartments().clear();
        getDepartments().addAll((Collection<? extends Department>)newValue);
        return;
      case EmployeePackage.COMPANY__EMPLOYEES:
        getEmployees().clear();
        getEmployees().addAll((Collection<? extends Employee>)newValue);
        return;
      case EmployeePackage.COMPANY__NAME:
        setName((String)newValue);
        return;
    }
    super.eSet(featureID, newValue);
  }

  @Override
  public void eUnset(int featureID)
  {
    switch (featureID)
    {
      case EmployeePackage.COMPANY__DEPARTMENTS:
        getDepartments().clear();
        return;
      case EmployeePackage.COMPANY__EMPLOYEES:
        getEmployees().clear();
        return;
      case EmployeePackage.COMPANY__NAME:
        setName(NAME_EDEFAULT);
        return;
    }
    super.eUnset(featureID);
  }

  @Override
  public boolean eIsSet(int featureID)
  {
    switch (featureID)
    {
      case EmployeePackage.COMPANY__DEPARTMENTS:
        return departments != null && !departments.isEmpty();
      case EmployeePackage.COMPANY__EMPLOYEES:
        return employees != null && !employees.isEmpty();
      case EmployeePackage.COMPANY__NAME:
        return NAME_EDEFAULT == null ? name != null : !NAME_EDEFAULT.equals(name);
    }
    return super.eIsSet(featureID);
  }

  @Override
  public String toString()
  {
    if (eIsProxy()) return super.toString();

    StringBuilder result = new StringBuilder(super.toString());
    result.append(" (name: ");
    result.append(name);
    result.append(')');
    return result.toString();
  }
